Let's compute exact Δg_p for h_i += d, h_j -= d:
Term t = h_n - h_n h_{n+p}. If only i affected as first: Δ = d - d h_{i+p} (if j not second). If only i as second: term h_{i-p} - h_{i-p}h_i; Δ = - h_{i-p} d? Actually h_i increases d: quadratic product h_{i-p}(h_i+d), so Δ = -h_{i-p} d. If both? Terms:

Maybe a simpler high-impact idea: Use gradient-based continuous relaxation with random restarts at finer resolution. Let's outline robust code functions:

- `c5_values(h,n)`: returns c = np.correlate(h,1-h,'full')*dx (array length 2n-1)
- `c5_max(h,n)`: max.
- `soft_obj_grad(h, n, alpha, mode=1)`: returns F, grad. F maybe smooth max: M + logsum/alpha. Use shift by max to avoid overflow. If alpha small. Weight computation: e = alpha*(c-M); sumexp; p=exp(e)/sumexp.
  grad = alpha * weighted_c_grad(p, n). Use `weighted_c_grad(weights, n, h?)` but derivative uses h for B,C and weights. Need compute with h.
  Pseudocode:
```
def grad_c_weighted(h, weights, n):
    dx=2/n
    A=np.zeros(n); B=np.zeros(n); C=np.zeros(n)
    # weights length 2n-1, index offset = n-1. For idx, p=idx-(n-1)
    for idx,p in enumerate(range(-(n-1),n)):
       w=weights[idx]
       if w <= 1e-15: continue
       if p>=0:
          L=n-p
          if L<=0: continue
          A[:L]+=w
          B[:L]+=w*h[p:n]
          C[p:n]+=w*h[:L]
       else:
          q=-p; L=n-q
          A[q:n]+=w
          B[q:n]+=w*h[:L]
          C[:L]+=w*h[q:n]
    return dx*(A - B - C)
```
Check for p positive: c_p first domain n=0..n-p-1, second h[p:n]. For each first n, second index r=n+p = p..n-1. C[p:n] += w*h[n] (where n slice z[:L]). yes.

Implementing LP:
```
def linprog_active(h, n, active_p, step):
    # active_p list of lags p
    k=len(active_p)
    if k==0: return None
    A = np.zeros((k,n+1))
    b = np.zeros(k)
    for r,p in enumerate(active_p):
        a = grad_c_one(h,p,n)  # length n
        A[r,:n] = a
        A[r,n] = 1.0
    c_obj = np.zeros(n+1); c_obj[n] = -1.0
    bounds=[]
    for i in range(n):
        lo = max(-step, -h[i])
        hi = min(step, 1.0-h[i])
        if lo > hi + 1e-14: continue
        bounds.append((lo,hi))
    bounds.append((-step, step))  # m
    # equality sum δ=0: A_eq row, b_eq 0
    A_eq = np.zeros((1,n+1)); A_eq[0,:n]=1
    res = linprog(c_obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=np.array([0]), bounds=bounds, method='highs')
    if res.status==0:
      delta=res.x[:n]
      # m=res.x[n]
      if m > 1e-7: return delta, m
    return None
```
But if `bounds` excludes variables with lo>hi? All h in [0,1] so lo≤hi if step>0 (lo=-min(step,h), hi=min(step,1-h)). Good.
`linprog` objective maximize m. It will find m as large as possible; if constraints require m negative, objective -m positive. We accept m > small. But if there is feasible m positive very small, step may be tiny. We can line search.

However, constraints are `aδ + m <=0`. To require predicted c_p <= c0_p - m. But if c0_p differ (some active slightly below max), and we only require relative reduction m, inactive? The maximum predicted = max_{p active}(c0_p + a_pδ) <= max_active c0_p - m? Actually if c0_p=max - ε, constraint a_pδ + m<=0 => new ≤ max-ε-m. Good. But for p with c0 below max-ε? not included. Fine.
We want predicted new max lower than current max by at least m. Good.

But if active_set includes many lags at max, feasible m may be limited. If no m positive for top all, try smaller active sets: top1, top2, etc. But reducing only top1 may increase others; line search might not accept. We can try different active combinations: top 1, top 3, top 5, top 10, top 20, all within tol. For each active, LP. We can also include a weighted sum objective instead of min max to find descent. Line search:
```
def line_search(h, delta, current_c):
   for tau in [1.0,0.8,0.5,0.3,0.1,0.05,0.01]:
       hnew = h + tau*delta
       if any out of [0,1] or abs(sum-target)>1e-8: continue
       cnew = c5_max(hnew,n)
       if cnew < current_c - 1e-12:
           return hnew,cnew,tau
   return None
```
Since δ from LP is linearized; if quadratic, smaller tau safer. If full step fail, shrink.

After LP step, recompute active. Repeat.

But LP constraints use gradients at current h, not including second-order; for large step (0.02) quadratic may invalidate. We can use step=0.005 initially, line search smaller. m scale dx*step ~0.01*0.005=5e-5. Need reduce total 7e-5, maybe one or two steps. Use step=0.01-0.02.

  c=c5_values; maxc=max
  active=[idx for idx,cval in enumerate(c) if cval >= maxc - 1e-4 *?]
  if len(active)>30: sort by c desc take 30
  improved=False

     for k in [1,3,5,10,20]:
       if k>len(active): continue
       active_sub=active[:k] # maybe by c
       res=linprog...
       if res and line_search success:
          h=hnew; improved=True; break
     if improved: break
  if not improved: break
```

def to_binary(h,n):
   # choose top n/2 values as 1, tie random? But sum exact.
   idx=np.argsort(h)[::-1]
   b=np.zeros(n); b[idx[:n//2]]=1
   return b
```

def binary_local_search(b,n,deadline,improve_only=True):
   maxc = int(np.max(np.correlate(b,1-b,'full')))
   ones = np.flatnonzero(b==1); zeros = np.flatnonzero(b==0)
   while time<deadline:
      # pick active lags maybe top
      improved=False
      # First-improvement or best-improvement over a random subset? Full best-improvement 2500 pairs evaluate correlate maybe okay.
      best_delta=None; best_newmax=inf
      # Use active constraints pruning: for each pair, quickly check active lags? Could just full.
      for i_idx in range(len(ones)):
        i=ones[i_idx]
        for j_idx in range(len(zeros)):
          j=zeros[j_idx]
          # if i==j impossible
          b[i]=0; b[j]=1
          newmax = max correlate ...
          if newmax < maxc: accept... break loops
          b[i]=1; b[j]=0
      # if no improve, try simulated annealing or break
```

Let's design full `run`:
```
def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
  import time
  t0=time.time(); deadline=t0+budget_s-3
  rng=np.random.RandomState(seed)
  # load parent
  try: parent=np.asarray(initial_h_values,float).copy(); n_parent=len(parent)
  except: parent=None; n_parent=200
  best_h=None; best_c=inf
  if parent is not None:
      best_h=project_feasible(parent, n_parent)
      best_c=compute_c5(best_h,n_parent)
  else:
      best_h=np.full(100,0.5); best_c=compute
  # MILP exploration
  if time.time() < deadline - 30:
      # try n values dynamically
      n_list=[20,30,40,50,60,80,100,120]
      # Maybe skip n_parent if not list? include n_parent if not
      for n in n_list:
          remaining=deadline-time.time()
          if remaining < 30: break
          # allocation: small n smaller time, large n more if current best high
          if n <=40: alloc=25
          elif n <=60: alloc=45
          elif n <=100: alloc=60
          else: alloc=50
          if remaining < alloc+5: alloc=max(10, remaining-5)
          # If best_c already much lower than target, reduce target? We can still run if can improve.
          # target upper bound count:
          Tcurrent = int(np.ceil(best_c*n/2.0 - 1e-12))? For objective upper set min(Tcurrent-1? We need improve; if current best not from this n, upper count can be floor(best_c*n/2) +1. Need define `binary_init(n, best_h, rng)`:
- If best_h exists and len>0: x_new=linspace, h_interp=interp; add small noise; choose top K values to set 1. To avoid deterministic block, use noise. `h_interp = np.interp(x_new, np.linspace(0,2,len(best_h)), best_h)`. thresholds.
```
def binary_from_continuous(hc, rng):
   n=len(hc); K=n//2
   scores = hc + rng.normal(0,0.1,n)
   idx=np.argsort(scores)[::-1][:K]
   b=np.zeros(n); b[idx]=1
   return b
```

Let H(i)=1+i. Z(i,j)=1+n+i*n+j.
Pair constraints for all i,j:
- if i==j: z <=0 => row z=0. Could instead set bounds (0,0) for diagonal z, no row. Diagonal variables we can fix by bounds 0, avoid constraints. Off-diag only 3 rows.
- z - H_i <= 0.

- z - H_i + H_j <= 0 (equiv z >= h_i - h_j). Check if h_i=1,h_j=1: z-1+1=z<=0 -> z=0. If h_i=0,h_j=0: z<=0. If h_i=1,h_j=0: z-1<=0, plus z>=? z -1 +0 <=0 => z<=1, and z>=? Actually third row is -? Constraints:
(1) z ≤ h_i -> z - h_i ≤0.
(2) z ≤ 1 - h_j -> z + h_j ≤1.
(3) z ≥ h_i - h_j -> -z + h_i - h_j ≤0? Multiply -1: z - h_i + h_j ≥0 -> -z + h_i - h_j ≤0. Yes row with -z + h_i - h_j <=0. If h_i=1,h_j=0: -z +1 <=0 => z>=1. Good. If h_i=0,h_j=1: -z -1 <=0 holds. So row: -Z + H_i - H_j <=0. Good.

Pseudo:
```
def milp_find(n, time_limit, T_upper, h_init=None):
    import scipy.optimize as opt, scipy.sparse as sp
    K=n//2; V=1+n+n*n
    row=[]; col=[]; val=[]; r=0
    def add(row_idx, items, lb, ub, lbarr?) ... but cannot closure? We can define top-level helper `append_constraint(rows,cols,vals,r,items)`? It would take lists, okay.
```

Function `compute_c5_values(h,n)`:
```
def compute_c5_values(h, n):
    dx=2.0/n
    return np.correlate(h, 1.0-h, mode='full')*dx
```

`compute_c5(h,n)`: float max.

`project_feasible(h,n)`: binary search shift. Let's implement robust with residual adjustment.
```
def project_feasible(h, n):
    x=np.clip(np.asarray(h,dtype=float),0.0,1.0)
    target=n/2.0
    s=x.sum()
    if abs(s-target)<1e-12:
       return x
    lo=-1.0; hi=1.0
    # If s>target, need lower λ? f(λ)=sum clip(x+λ). f decreasing with lower? f increasing with λ.
    # We can binary universally: maintain lo gives f<=target? hi gives f>=target? At lo=-1 f=0 <= target (target>0). At hi=1 f=n >=target.
    for _ in range(80):
       mid=(lo+hi)/2
       f=np.clip(x+mid,0.0,1.0).sum()
       if f < target: lo=mid
       else: hi=mid
    y=np.clip(x+(lo+hi)/2,0.0,1.0)
    diff=target-y.sum()
    if abs(diff)<1e-12: return y
    # adjust interior or any
    if diff >0:
       idx=np.flatnonzero(y<1.0)
    else:
       idx=np.flatnonzero(y>0.0)
    if len(idx)>0:
       room = np.ones_like(diff scalar?) We can distribute proportionally to room.
       if diff>0:
          room=1.0-y[idx]
          tot=room.sum()
          if tot>0: y[idx]+=diff*room/tot
       else:
          room=y[idx]
          tot=room.sum()
          if tot>0: y[idx]+=diff*room/tot
    # final tiny adjust one variable
    diff=target-y.sum()
    if abs(diff)>0:
       # find variable not at bound in direction
       if diff>0:
          idx=np.flatnonzero(y<1.0)
       else:
          idx=np.flatnonzero(y>0.0)
       if len(idx)>0:
          # choose middle value closest to target
          i=idx[np.argmin(np.abs(diff - (1.0 if diff>0 else 0.0) - y[idx]? simpler pick max room)]]
          # adjust within bounds
          y[idx[0]] += diff if within else clamp and residual
       y=np.clip(y,0,1)
    return y
```
Need avoid O(n) loop inside 80? okay. But residual after binary very small; might not need. Use previous iterative proportional simpler? Binary search yields exact within 1e-12. Fine.
Could break if x already exact.

Maybe if x all 0/1 and target, binary search midpoint yields many at bounds and exact? yes. Potential issue: `clip(x+mid)` vectorized each iter, 80* n (n small) fine.

`soft_objective_grad(h,n,alpha)`:
Need return value and gradient. Use c = compute_c5_values. M = c.max(); e = alpha*(c-M); sumexp = sum(exp(e)) maybe underflow; weights=exp(e)/sumexp. If sumexp==0? set weights uniform? For alpha large and M, one max weight1. objective = M + (1/alpha)*log(sumexp). Gradient = alpha * weighted_grad. If alpha=0? avoid. Return objective.
Compute weighted_grad via `weighted_lag_gradient(h, weights, n)` top-level.
```
def weighted_lag_gradient(h, weights, n):
    dx=2.0/n
    A=np.zeros(n); B=np.zeros(n); C=np.zeros(n)
    offset=n-1
    # weights length 2n-1 in np.correlate full index order: idx 0 lag=-(n-1)
    for idx in range(2*n-1):
       w=weights[idx]
       if w <= 1e-14: continue
       p=idx - (n-1)
       if p >= 0:
          L=n-p
          if L<=0: continue
          A[:L] += w
          B[:L] += w*h[p:n]
          C[p:n] += w*h[:L]
       else:
          q=-p; L=n-q
          A[q:n] += w
          B[q:n] += w*h[:L]
          C[:L] += w*h[q:n]
    return dx*(A - B - C)
```
Check p positive L=n-p (if p=n-1, L=1, fine). p negative q, L=n-q. Good.
Potential optimize: If p=0, A all w, B all w*h, C all w*h, grad=w dx(1-2h). Good.

`soft_objective(x,n,alpha)` top-level for minimize:
```
def soft_objective(x,n,alpha):
    val,grad = soft_objective_grad(x,n,alpha)
    return val
def soft_jacobian(x,n,alpha):
    val,grad = soft_objective_grad(x,n,alpha)
    return grad
```
But computing c twice if separate? In SLSQP, objective and jac called separately; we can recompute. To avoid duplicate, maybe not critical. Could cache? Not with top-level no global? Could use global cache but messy. Recompute okay.

We can clip in functions? If we clip x for objective but not gradient, inconsistent. SLSQP should keep inside. But numerical may go outside 1e-12. We can not clip to avoid inconsistency. But weights can overflow if h huge; not.

```
def slsqp_optimize(h0,n,alpha,maxiter,deadline=None,ftol=1e-12):
    from scipy.optimize import minimize, LinearConstraint? SLSQP constraints dict.
    cons=[{'type':'eq','fun':_sum_con,'jac':_sum_con_jac,'args':(n,)}]
    bounds=[(0.0,1.0)]*n
    try:
      res=minimize(soft_objective, h0, args=(n,alpha), jac=soft_jacobian, method='SLSQP', bounds=bounds, constraints=cons, options={'maxiter':maxiter,'ftol':ftol,'disp':False})
      if res.x is not None: return project_feasible(res.x,n)
    except Exception: pass
    return project_feasible(h0,n)
```

`grad_one_lag(h,p,n)` top-level as derived.
```
def grad_one_lag(h,p,n):
    g=np.zeros(n); dx=2/n
    if p>=0:
       L=n-p
       if L>0:
          g[:L] += dx*(1.0 - h[p:n])
          g[p:n] -= dx*h[:L]
    else:
       q=-p; L=n-q
       g[q:n] += dx*(1.0 - h[:L])
       g[:L] -= dx*h[q:n]
    return g
```
Check p=0: g[:n]+=dx*(1-h); g[:n]-=dx*h => dx(1-2h). Good.

Line search: need `h + tau*delta` feasible. Since delta bounds ensure, but numerical. We'll check.
```
def try_lp_direction(h, delta, current_c):
   taud=tau*delta
   hnew=h+taud
   if np.any(hnew<-1e-10) or np.any(hnew>1+1e-10): continue
   if abs(hnew.sum() - len(h)/2)>1e-7: continue
   cnew=compute_c5_values(hnew,n); mn=cnew.max()
   if mn < current_c - 1e-12: return hnew,mn
```
Need avoid closure. Top-level `lineseach_lp(h,delta,n,current_c)`. It can loop tau and return (hnew,cnew) or None. But it needs compute values; top-level. We'll implement:
```
def lp_line_search(h, delta, n, current_c):
   for tau in [1.0,0.7,0.4,0.2,0.1,0.05,0.02,0.01]:
      hnew=h+tau*delta
      if float(np.max(hnew))>1+1e-9 or float(np.min(hnew))<-1e-9: continue
      if abs(hnew.sum()-n/2.0)>1e-6: continue
      cnew=compute_c5(hnew,n)
      if cnew < current_c - 1e-13:
         return hnew, cnew
   return None
```
Use compute_c5 not values (less memory). Good.

def run(seed=42,budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
  t0=time.time(); deadline=t0+budget_s-3; rng=np.random.RandomState(seed)
  # initial parent
  try: parent=np.asarray(initial_h_values,float).copy(); n_parent=len(parent)
  except: parent=None; n_parent=200
  if parent is not None and n_parent>0:
     best_h=project_feasible(parent,n_parent); best_c=compute_c5(best_h,n_parent)
  else:
     n0=100; best_h=np.full(n0,0.5); best_c=compute_c5(best_h,n0)
  # MIP exploration for improving binary
  if time.time() < deadline - 40:
     n_seq=[20,30,40,50,60,80,100,120]
     # skip if n > int((best_c? no))
     for n in n_seq:
        rem=deadline-time.time()
        if rem<40: break
        # complexity allocation
        if n<=20: alloc=20
        elif n<=30: alloc=35
        elif n<=40: alloc=50
        elif n<=60: alloc=70
        elif n<=80: alloc=80
        elif n<=100: alloc=90
        else: alloc=80
        if rem < alloc + 10: alloc=max(15, rem-10)
        # T limit for strict improvement
        limit = int(np.floor(best_c * n / 2.0 - 1e-9))
        if limit < 0: continue
        # lower bound? average maybe ceil(n/2*(n/2)/(2n-1))? If limit < ceil(avg) skip? The avg count lower bound: n/(8?) Use average count = (n*n/4)/(2n-1). max>=ceil(avg). But continuous not binary. For MIP binary, if limit < ceil(avg) skip.
        avg_count = (n*n/4.0)/(2*n-1)
        if limit < int(np.ceil(avg_count - 1e-12)): continue
        # Generate initial candidates maybe; but build only if time.
        # If best_h from continuous, use threshold; also random.
        h_inits=[]
        h_inits.append(binary_from_continuous(project? best_h, n, rng))
        for _ in range(3): h_inits.append(random_binary(n,rng))
        # if any max_count <= limit, x0_feasible = one with smallest
        x0 = None; mincnt=10**9
        for hi in h_inits:
           cnt=int(np.max(np.correlate(hi,1-hi,'full')))
           if cnt<mincnt: mincnt=cnt; x0=hi
           if cnt<=limit: break
        # If x0 count <= limit, we already found an improving solution without MIP! update best? Yes if cnt<=limit, C=cnt*2/n < best_c by limit definition.
        if mincnt <= limit:
            cnew = mincnt*2.0/n
            if cnew < best_c:
                best_h=x0.astype(float); best_c=cnew
                # We can still try MIP lower? Not now.
        else:
            # call MIP only if limit is not too high? For n large maybe.
            resh = milp_feasible_binary(n, limit, min(alloc, rem-20), x0)
            if resh is not None:
                cnew=compute_c5(resh,n) # should <= limit*dx
                if cnew < best_c:
                   best_h=resh.astype(float); best_c=cnew
        # if found best_c <= 0.30 maybe continue to n? yes
  # continuous optimization on best
  if best_h is not None and time.time() < deadline - 20:
     n=len(best_h)
     # If n very small (<=60), SLSQP fast. If n large, limited.
     if n <= 60:
        # use binary MIP maybe already; run optimize_known full
        best_h,best_c=optimize_known(best_h,n, min(deadline-5, time.time()+200), rng)
     else:
        # do LP polish on parent (200/120) with limited time
        if time.time() < deadline - 10:
           best_h,best_c=active_lp_polish(best_h,n, deadline-5, max_iter=200)
        if time.time() < deadline - 10:
           # one SLSQP alpha 10000 maxiter=100
           h2=slsqp_optimize(best_h,n,10000,maxiter=120)
           c2=compute_c5(h2,n)
           if c2<best_c: best_h,best_c=h2,c2
           best_h,best_c=active_lp_polish(best_h,n, deadline-5, max_iter=100)
     # pair search final if n <=80
     if n <= 80 and time.time() < deadline - 5:
        best_h,best_c=pair_search(best_h,n,deadline-1,[0.01,0.005,0.002,0.001],radius=0)
     elif len(best_h) <= 200 and time.time() < deadline-5:
        best_h,best_c=near_pair_search(best_h,n,deadline-1,[0.01,0.005,0.002],radius=15)
     best_h=project_feasible(best_h,n); best_c=compute_c5(best_h,n)
  # If no improvement and parent exists, maybe final LP on parent with remaining? Already.
  return best_h, float(best_c), int(len(best_h))

Need define `random_binary(n,rng)`:
```
def random_binary(n, rng):
   idx = rng.choice(n, size=n//2, replace=False)
   b=np.zeros(n); b[idx]=1; return b
```
`binary_from_continuous(h_best, n, rng)`: if h_best length, interp, threshold top with noise. If none, random.
```
def binary_from_continuous(hc, n, rng):
    if hc is None or len(hc)==0: return random_binary(n,rng)
    if len(hc)==n: x=hc
    else: x=np.interp(np.linspace(0,2,n), np.linspace(0,2,len(hc)), hc)
    scores = x + rng.normal(0,0.1,n)
    idx=np.argsort(scores)[::-1][:n//2]
    b=np.zeros(n); b[idx]=1.0; return b
```

Let's implement `optimize_known` more concretely:
```
def optimize_known(h0, n, deadline, rng, aggressive=True):
    h=project_feasible(h0,n); best_c=compute_c5(h,n)
    # 1. LP polish for initial n using limited time
    if time.time() < deadline - 10:
       lp_deadline = min(deadline - 30 if aggressive else deadline - 5, time.time() + min(120, max(20, (deadline-time.time())*0.25)))
       h2,c2=active_lp_polish(h,n,lp_deadline,max_iter=300)
       if c2<best_c: h,best_c=h2,c2
    # 2. gradient polish maybe (20 steps)
    if time.time() < deadline - 10:
       h2,c2=gradient_polish(h,n,min(deadline-10, time.time()+60), alphas=[2000,10000,50000])
       update
    # 3. SLSQP few alphas if n <=150 or time
    if time.time() < deadline - 20:
       if n <= 150: seq=[500,2000,10000,50000]
       else: seq=[2000,10000]
       for a in seq:
          if time.time() > deadline-12: break
          maxiter=80 if n<=80 else (120 if n<=150 else 80)
          h2=slsqp_optimize(h,n,a,maxiter=maxiter)
          c2=compute_c5(h2,n)
          if c2<best_c-1e-12: h,best_c=h2,c2
          else: maybe stop if no improve for 2?
    # 4 final LP
    if time.time() < deadline - 5:
       h2,c2=active_lp_polish(h,n,deadline-1,max_iter=100)
       update
    # 5 pair search if n small
    if n <= 80 and time.time() < deadline - 3:
       h,best_c=pair_search(h,n,deadline-1,[0.01,0.005,0.002,0.001],radius=0)
    elif n <= 200 and time.time() < deadline -3:
       h,best_c=near_pair_search(h,n,deadline-1,[0.01,0.005,0.002],radius=10)
    h=project_feasible(h,n); best_c=compute_c5(h,n)
    return h,best_c
```
But `active_lp_polish` may itself run to deadline if many steps; pass deadline.

`milp_feasible_binary` code details:
```
def milp_feasible_binary(n, T_limit, time_limit, h_init=None):
    K=n//2
    if T_limit < 0: return None
    # feasible lower bound: max_count >= ceil(K*(n-K)//(2n-1))? If T_limit < ceil(...), return None quickly
    avg = (K*K)/(2*n-1)
    if T_limit < int(np.ceil(avg - 1e-12)): return None
    if h_init is not None:
       h_init=np.asarray(h_init,dtype=float)
       # ensure binary and sum: project? It should be binary. If max_count <= T_limit, return h_init
       cnt = int(np.max(np.correlate(h_init, 1-h_init, mode='full')))
       if cnt <= T_limit:
           return h_init
    try:
      import scipy.optimize as sopt; import scipy.sparse as sp
    except Exception: return None
    m=n
    zsize=n*n
    V=n+zsize
    # Build arrays
    rows=[]; cols=[]; vals=[]; lbs=[]; ubs=[]
    r=0
    # sum constraint
    for i in range(n): rows.append(r); cols.append(i); vals.append(1.0)
    lbs.append(float(K)); ubs.append(float(K)); r+=1
    # lag constraints
    for p in range(-(n-1), n):
       if p==0: continue
       rows.append(r); cols.append(-T_index?) No T variable. Wait we removed T variable; constraints sum z <= T_limit, no T. So row just sum z, ub=T_limit.
       start=max(0,-p); end=min(n, n-p)
       for i in range(start,end):
          j=i+p
          if 0<=j<n:
             rows.append(r); cols.append(n + i*n + j); vals.append(1.0)
       lbs.append(-np.inf); ubs.append(float(T_limit)); r+=1
    # pair constraints offdiag
    for i in range(n):
       hi=i
       zrow_base=n + i*n
       for j in range(n):
          if i==j: continue
          z=zrow_base+j; hj=j
          # z - hi <=0
          rows.append(r); cols.append(z); vals.append(1.0)
          rows.append(r); cols.append(hi); vals.append(-1.0)
          lbs.append(-np.inf); ubs.append(0.0); r+=1
          # z + hj <=1
          rows.append(r); cols.append(z); vals.append(1.0)
          rows.append(r); cols.append(hj); vals.append(1.0)
          lbs.append(-np.inf); ubs.append(1.0); r+=1
          # -z + hi - hj <=0
          rows.append(r); cols.append(z); vals.append(-1.0)
          rows.append(r); cols.append(hi); vals.append(1.0)
          rows.append(r); cols.append(hj); vals.append(-1.0)
          lbs.append(-np.inf); ubs.append(0.0); r+=1
    A=sp.coo_matrix((vals,(rows,cols)), shape=(r,V)).tocsr()
    cons=sopt.LinearConstraint(A, np.asarray(lbs), np.asarray(ubs))
    c=np.zeros(V)
    bounds=[]
    for i in range(n): bounds.append((0.0,1.0))
    for i in range(n):
       for j in range(n):
          if i==j: bounds.append((0.0,0.0))
          else: bounds.append((0.0,1.0))
    integ=np.zeros(V,dtype=int); integ[:n]=1
    # x0
    x0=None
    if h_init is not None:
       # If infeasible due T? If count > T no. If count <= T returned. So no x0.
       pass
    # If T_limit >= n? maybe feasible. x0 none.
    options={'time_limit': int(max(1,time_limit)), 'mip_rel_gap': 0.0, 'mip_abs_gap': 0.0}
    try:
       res=sopt.milp(c, constraints=cons, integrality=integ, bounds=bounds, options=options)
    except Exception:
       # retry smaller if time? return None
       return None
    if res is not None and res.x is not None:
       hres=np.rint(res.x[:n]).astype(int)
       if hres.sum()!=K:
          # try fix by selecting K largest? if off by small
          # If sum too high, set some 1 to0; if too low.
       # check max_count <=T_limit
       cnt=int(np.max(np.correlate(hres, 1-hres, mode='full')))
       if cnt <= T_limit: return hres.astype(float)
    return None
```

def pair_search(h,n,deadline,deltas,radius=30):
    h=project_feasible(h,n); best=compute_c5
    for d in deltas:
      improved_any=False
      for _pass in range(50):
        if time.time()>deadline: return
        improved=False
        if n <= 80:
           for i in range(n):
             if time... return
             for j in range(i+1,n):
               # two directions if values allow and not binary? We'll use continuous value shifts.
               hi=h[i]+d; hj=h[j]-d
               if 0<=hi<=1 and 0<=hj<=1:
                  h[i],h[j]=hi,hj
                  c=compute_c5(h,n)
                  if c < best - 1e-13: best=c; improved=True
                  else: restore
               hi2=h[i]-d; hj2=h[j]+d
               if 0<=...:
                  ... if no improve restore
               # if improved, break? To keep simple, after improvement continue inner; but we changed h and continue. okay.
        else:
           for i... for j in i-radius..i+radius...
        if not improved: break
      # after deltas, maybe loop again? no.
    return project_feasible(h,n), compute_c5
```

Now, gradient_polish details. We can use exact c and soft gradient. Need no closures. Top-level:
```
def gradient_polish(h, n, deadline, alphas=(2000,10000,50000)):
   h=project_feasible(h,n); best=compute_c5(h,n)
   for alpha in alphas:
      for _ in range(200):
         if time.time()>deadline: break
         val,grad=soft_objective_grad(h,n,alpha)
         gmax=float(np.max(np.abs(grad)))
         if gmax < 1e-12: break
         improved=False
         for step in [0.02,0.01,0.005,0.002,0.001,0.0005]:
             hnew=project_feasible(h - (step/gmax)*grad, n)
             cnew=compute_c5(hnew,n)
             if cnew < best - 1e-13:
                h=hnew; best=cnew; improved=True; break
         if not improved: break
   return h,best
```
This may be slow: each iteration gradient O(n^2) plus 6 c5. For n=200, 3 alphas*200*6=3600 correlates (144M ops) maybe 10s; okay. But if no improvement early break. Good.

Generate random candidates:
```
def candidate_binaries(n, best_h, rng, count=8):
   cands=[]
   cands.append(binary_from_continuous(best_h,n,rng))
   for _ in range(count-1): cands.append(random_binary(n,rng))
   # maybe block pattern deterministic: for period in [3,4,5,7,8,11,13]: create pattern 1^(p/2)0^(p/2) repeated; if n multiple adjust top K? Let's add patterns to help.
   for period in [3,4,5,7,8,11,13,17]:
       x=np.arange(n)
       b=( (x % period) < (period//2) ).astype(float)
       # adjust sum to K by flipping lowest/high? Need exact.
       b=adjust_binary_sum(b,n)
       cands.append(b)
   return cands
```

`compute_c5_values`:
```
def compute_c5_values(h, n):
    dx = 2.0 / float(n)
    return np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full") * dx
```
`compute_c5`: return float(np.max(...)). If n=0? not.

`project_feasible` robust:
```
def project_feasible(h, n):
    x = np.clip(np.asarray(h, dtype=float), 0.0, 1.0)
    target = n / 2.0
    s = float(x.sum())
    if abs(s - target) < 1e-12:
        return x
    lo = -1.0
    hi = 1.0
    # ensure f(lo)<=target<=f(hi) always (target between 0,n)
    for _ in range(80):
        mid = 0.5*(lo+hi)
        f = float(np.clip(x + mid, 0.0, 1.0).sum())
        if f < target:
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid
    y = np.clip(x + 0.5*(lo+hi), 0.0, 1.0)
    diff = target - float(y.sum())
    if abs(diff) > 1e-11:
        if diff > 0:
            idx = np.flatnonzero(y < 1.0)
        else:
            idx = np.flatnonzero(y > 0.0)
        if idx.size > 0:
            if diff > 0:
                room = 1.0 - y[idx]
                tot = float(room.sum())
                if tot > 1e-15:
                    y[idx] += diff * room / tot
            else:
                room = y[idx]
                tot = float(room.sum())
                if tot > 1e-15:
                    y[idx] += diff * room / tot
    y = np.clip(y, 0.0, 1.0)
    # final exact adjust one variable
    diff = target - float(y.sum())
    if abs(diff) > 1e-14:
        if diff > 0:
            idx = np.flatnonzero(y < 1.0)
        else:
            idx = np.flatnonzero(y > 0.0)
        if idx.size > 0:
            # choose variable closest to requiring adjustment
            if diff > 0:
                idx2 = idx[np.argmax(1.0 - y[idx])]
            else:
                idx2 = idx[np.argmax(y[idx])]
            add = diff if diff > 0 else diff
            y[idx2] += add
            y[idx2] = min(1.0, max(0.0, y[idx2]))
    return y
```
The final adjust could overshoot sum if multiple? If idx chosen with enough room. diff tiny. If not enough, clip and residual maybe. Could include a loop. But diff after binary should be <1e-12. Fine.

Potential issue: For x all 0/1, binary search with 80 iterations on vector n maybe returns midpoint where f exactly target? It may land with many fractions due x+mid clipped. But sum target. Good.

`weighted_lag_gradient`: Use loops. Need weights sum maybe 1. If all weights zero? Weights from softmax positive. But due underflow, if sumexp overflows? We compute shifted by max. For alpha large, e max 0, others <=0. sumexp>=1. Good.
```
def weighted_lag_gradient(h, weights, n):
    dx = 2.0 / float(n)
    A = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    B = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    C = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    off = n - 1
    for idx in range(2 * n - 1):
        w = float(weights[idx])
        if w <= 1e-15:
            continue
        p = idx - off
        if p >= 0:
            L = n - p
            if L <= 0:
                continue
            A[:L] += w
            B[:L] += w * h[p:n]
            C[p:n] += w * h[:L]
        else:
            q = -p
            L = n - q
            A[q:n] += w
            B[q:n] += w * h[:L]
            C[:L] += w * h[q:n]
    return dx * (A - B - C)
```
Need h slice inside B `h[p:n]` length L. okay.

`soft_objective_grad`:
```
def soft_objective_grad(h,n,alpha):
    c=compute_c5_values(h,n)
    m=float(np.max(c))
    if alpha <=0: return m, np.zeros(n)
    z=alpha*(c-m)
    maxz=float(np.max(z)) # 0
    ex=np.exp(z-maxz)
    s=float(np.sum(ex))
    if s <=0 or not np.isfinite(s): weights=np.zeros(len(c)); weights[int(np.argmax(c))]=1.0
    else: weights=ex/s
    grad=alpha*weighted_lag_gradient(h, weights, n)
    val=m + float(np.log(s))/alpha  # since maxz=0? Actually logsumexp = maxz + log(s); we shifted by m? We set z=alpha(c-m), maxz=0. logsumexp(alpha(c-m))=log(s). soft = m + log(s)/alpha. Good.
    return val, grad
```
If c has nan due? h finite. Good.

`soft_objective` and `soft_jacobian`: Top-level. They recompute.
```
def soft_objective(x,n,alpha):
    v,g=soft_objective_grad(x,n,alpha); return v
def soft_jacobian(x,n,alpha):
    v,g=soft_objective_grad(x,n,alpha); return g
```

`slsqp_optimize`: Need import minimize top-level. If `minimize` not imported? top. Use `try`.
```
def slsqp_optimize(h0, n, alpha, maxiter=100):
    h0=project_feasible(h0,n)
    bounds=[(0.0,1.0)] * n
    cons=[{"type": "eq", "fun": sum_constraint, "jac": sum_constraint_jac, "args": (n,)}]
    try:
       res = minimize(soft_objective, h0, args=(n, alpha), method="SLSQP",
                      bounds=bounds, constraints=cons, jac=soft_jacobian,
                      options={"maxiter": int(maxiter), "ftol": 1e-12, "disp": 0})
       if getattr(res,"x",None) is not None:
           return project_feasible(res.x, n)
    except Exception:
       pass
    return h0
```
The jac function signature `soft_jacobian(x,n,alpha)` matches. Constraint `sum_constraint_jac(x,n)` returns ones. For SLSQP, constraint fun call `sum_constraint(x,n)`. Good.

`grad_one_lag`:
```
def grad_one_lag(h,p,n):
    g=np.zeros(n,dtype=float); dx=2.0/n
    if p >=0:
       L=n-p
       if L>0:
          g[:L] += dx*(1.0 - h[p:n])
          g[p:n] -= dx*h[:L]
    else:
       q=-p; L=n-q
       if L>0:
          g[q:n] += dx*(1.0 - h[:L])
          g[:L] -= dx*h[q:n]
    return g
```
Check p=n? not; lags range -(n-1)..n-1.

`lp_line_search`:
```
def lp_line_search(h, delta, n, current_c):
    target = n / 2.0
    for tau in (1.0,0.7,0.4,0.2,0.1,0.05,0.02,0.01,0.005):
        hnew = h + tau * delta
        if float(np.max(hnew)) > 1.0 + 1e-9 or float(np.min(hnew)) < -1e-9:
             continue
        if abs(float(hnew.sum()) - target) > 1e-6:
             continue
        cnew = compute_c5(hnew,n)
        if cnew < current_c - 1e-13:
             return hnew, cnew
    return None
```

`active_lp_polish`:
```
def active_lp_polish(h, n, deadline, max_iter=200):
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
    h=project_feasible(h,n)
    cval=compute_c5_values(h,n)
    best_c=float(np.max(cval))
    steps=[0.02,0.01,0.005,0.002,0.001]
    for step in steps:
       for _ in range(max_iter):
          if time.time() > deadline:
              return h,best_c
          maxc=float(np.max(cval))
          # dynamic active tolerance
          tol=max(1e-7, 0.001) maybe too high? We want active near max. Use tol=max(1e-6, 1e-4*max(1,maxc))? Since C ~0.38, tol 0.002? 0.002 corresponds 10e-6? Actually maxc 0.38, 0.001 maybe. Previous improvements small. Use 1e-4? That includes lags within 0.0001; few. If too few, k adapt.
          active = np.where(cval >= maxc - 1e-4)[0]
          if active.size == 0:
              active = np.array([int(np.argmax(cval))])
          active = active[np.argsort(cval[active])][::-1]
          improved=False
          k_choices=[1,2,4,8,12,20, active.size]
          for k in k_choices:
             if k <=0: continue
             if k > active.size: continue
             if improved: break
             p_list=[int(idx - (n-1)) for idx in active[:k]]
             A=[]; b=[]
             ok=True
             for p in p_list:
                a=grad_one_lag(h,p,n)
                row=np.zeros(n+1); row[:n]=a; row[n]=1.0
                A.append(row); b.append(0.0)
             if len(A)==0: continue
             A=np.array(A)
             b=np.array(b)
             c_obj=np.zeros(n+1); c_obj[n] = -1.0
             bounds=[]
             for i in range(n):
                lo=max(-step, -h[i])
                hi=min(step, 1.0-h[i])
                if lo > hi + 1e-14:
                    ok=False; break
                bounds.append((lo,hi))
             if not ok: continue
             bounds.append((-step, step))
             A_eq=np.zeros((1,n+1)); A_eq[0,:n]=1.0
             try:
                res=linprog(c_obj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, A_eq=A_eq, b_eq=np.array([0.0]),
                            bounds=bounds, method="highs")
             except Exception:
                continue
             if res is not None and getattr(res,"success",False):
                delta=res.x[:n]
                m_pred=float(res.x[n])
                if delta is not None and np.all(np.isfinite(delta)) and m_pred > 1e-8:
                   found=lp_line_search(h, delta, n, best_c)
                   if found is not None:
                      h_new, c_new=found
                      # recompute values
                      cval=compute_c5_values(h_new,n)
                      best_c=c_new
                      h=h_new
                      improved=True
                      break
          if not improved:
             # try if no subset worked; break step outer
             # maybe if active all been tried, break
             if k_choices[-1] >= active.size:
                break
          if time.time() > deadline:
             break
       # after step, maybe recompute?
       if time.time() > deadline: break
    return project_feasible(h,n), compute_c5(h,n)
```
This code has a logical issue: The `for k` loop, if a k fails, continue to next k. If none improved, `improved` false. The `if not improved: if k_choices[-1]>=active.size: break` does nothing because it's after for; should break outer step loop. Need better structure. Use a `worked=False` per step. After for k, if not improved, break to next step? But if subset k failed due line search, maybe another step smaller? So break internal and continue outer step. We can simply after k loop if not improved: break (inner _ iteration), then step outer continues to smaller step. But for each step, inner loop should repeat until no improvement; if no improvement with this step, break. That's what we can do:
```
for step in steps:
  while True:
    if time>deadline: return
    ... try k ...
    if improved: continue
    break
```
But while True can infinite if improved keeps same? Objective decreases, finite.

       ... set improved=False; loop k; if improved: improved=True (local) break out k
       if improved:
          improved_in_step=True
          continue
       break
```
After for _, step ends. If improved_in_step and time, maybe keep same step? We already loop until no improvement. Good.
Need to exit k loop when improved. We'll code with `k_done` flag.

`gradient_polish`:
```
def gradient_polish(h,n,deadline, alphas):
   h=project_feasible(h,n); best=compute_c5(h,n)
   for alpha in alphas:
      for _ in range(80):
        if time.time()>deadline: return h,best
        _, grad = soft_objective_grad(h,n,alpha)
        gmax=float(np.max(np.abs(grad)))
        if gmax < 1e-14: break
        improved=False
        for target_step in [0.02,0.01,0.005,0.002,0.001,0.0005]:
           hnew=project_feasible(h - (target_step/gmax)*grad, n)
           cnew=compute_c5(hnew,n)
           if cnew < best - 1e-13:
              h=hnew; best=cnew; improved=True; break
        if not improved: break
   return project_feasible(h,n), compute_c5(h,n)
```
Could be expensive if project_feasible each step. okay.

`pair_search` as above. Need ensure no infinite. We'll use a helper top-level `try_pair_move`? Could be closure? We'll inline.
```
def pair_search(h, n, deadline, deltas, radius=25):
   h=project_feasible(h,n); best_c=compute_c5(h,n)
   for d in deltas:
      for _pass in range(30):
         if time.time() > deadline: break
         improved=False
         if n <= 80:
            for i in range(n):
               if time.time() > deadline: break
               for j in range(i+1,n):
                  # dir 1
                  hi=h[i]+d; hj=h[j]-d
                  if 0.0 <= hi <=1.0 and 0.0 <= hj <=1.0:
                     h[i]=hi; h[j]=hj
                     c=compute_c5(h,n)
                     if c < best_c - 1e-13:
                        best_c=c; improved=True
                     else:
                        h[i]=hi-d; h[j]=hj+d
                  hi2=h[i]-d; hj2=h[j]+d
                  if 0.0 <= hi2 <=1.0 and 0.0 <= hj2 <=1.0:
                     h[i]=hi2; h[j]=hj2
                     c=compute_c5(h,n)
                     if c < best_c - 1e-13:
                        best_c=c; improved=True
                     else:
                        h[i]=hi2+d; h[j]=hj2-d
               if time.time() > deadline: break
         else:
            for i in range(n): ...
         if not improved: break
      if time.time() > deadline: break
   h=project_feasible(h,n); best_c=compute_c5(h,n)
   return h,best_c
```

Restoring after failed: If improved in dir1, h changed, best updated; then dir2 uses updated h (h[i] increased, h[j] decreased). That's okay? It may combine moves within same pass. But h[i] might be at new value; h[j]. Good. For dir1, if improved, we do not restore. For dir2, if failed, we restore from current to previous? We set hi2=h[i]-d where h[i] is new. If failed after applying hi2, restore h[i]=hi2+d, h[j]=hj2-d, which returns to current. Good.
For full pair, O(n^2) per pass. n=80 okay. For near, use radius and j range; avoid i==j. For n=200, radius 15, pairs ~2n*15=6000 per direction? 6000, okay.

`optimize_known`: Need use actual current time. We'll implement with careful allocations.
```
def optimize_known(h0, n, deadline, rng, aggressive=True):
    h=project_feasible(h0,n)
    best_c=compute_c5(h,n)
    # LP first
    if time.time() < deadline - 8:
       lp_deadline = min(deadline - 10, time.time() + 120)
       h2,c2=active_lp_polish(h,n,lp_deadline,max_iter=200)
       if c2 < best_c - 1e-14: h,best_c=h2,c2
    # gradient polish
    if time.time() < deadline - 10:
       gp_deadline = min(deadline - 10, time.time() + 60)
       h2,c2=gradient_polish(h,n,gp_deadline, alphas=[3000,10000,50000])
       update
    # SLSQP
    if time.time() < deadline - 15:
       if n <= 80: alphas=[1000,5000,20000,100000]; maxiter=[80,80,80,80]
       elif n <= 150: alphas=[3000,15000]; maxiter=[100,100]
       else: alphas=[5000]; maxiter=[80]
       for a in alphas:
          if time.time() > deadline - 12: break
          h2=slsqp_optimize(h,n,a,maxiter=maxiter)
          c2=compute_c5(h2,n)
          if c2 < best_c - 1e-13: h,best_c=h2,c2
          else:
             # if no improvement, maybe still continue? break if no improve after one?
             pass
    # final LP
    if time.time() < deadline - 4:
       h2,c2=active_lp_polish(h,n,deadline-1,max_iter=100)
       update
    # final pair
    if time.time() < deadline - 2:
       if n <= 80:
          h,best_c=pair_search(h,n,deadline-1,[0.01,0.005,0.002,0.001],radius=0,max_evals=200000)
       elif n <= 200:
          h,best_c=pair_search(h,n,deadline-1,[0.01,0.005,0.002],radius=10,max_evals=30000)
    return project_feasible(h,n), compute_c5(h,n)
```
SLSQP could be slow, but maxiter moderate. For n=200, 80 iter okay. For n=30, multiple alphas okay.

`adjust_binary_sum(b,n,rng)`:
```
def adjust_binary_sum(b,n,rng):
    b=(b>0.5).astype(float).copy()
    K=n//2
    s=int(b.sum())
    if s == K: return b
    if s > K:
       idx=np.flatnonzero(b==1.0)
       order=rng.permutation(idx.size)
       for t in range(s-K):
           b[idx[order[t]]]=0.0
    else:
       idx=np.flatnonzero(b==0.0)
       order=rng.permutation(idx.size)
       for t in range(K-s):
           b[idx[order[t]]]=1.0
    return b
```
If idx size less? Should not if K within [0,n]. Good.

`binary_from_continuous`: as above, uses `np.arange` maybe? For interpolation, if len hc 0. Use `np.linspace(0.0,2.0,len(hc))`. If len=1? fine.
```
def binary_from_continuous(hc,n,rng):
    if hc is None or len(hc)==0:
       return random_binary(n,rng)
    if len(hc)==n:
       x=hc.copy()
    else:
       x_old=np.linspace(0.0,2.0,len(hc))
       x_new=np.linspace(0.0,2.0,n)
       x=np.interp(x_new, x_old, hc)
    scores=x + rng.normal(0.0, 0.1, n)
    idx=np.argsort(scores)[::-1]
    K=n//2
    b=np.zeros(n,dtype=float); b[idx[:K]]=1.0
    return b
```

`random_binary`:
```
def random_binary(n,rng):
    idx=rng.choice(n, size=n//2, replace=False)
    b=np.zeros(n,dtype=float); b[idx]=1.0; return b
```

def candidate_binaries(n,best_h,rng,max_cands=12):
    cands=[]
    cands.append(binary_from_continuous(best_h,n,rng))
    # randoms
    for _ in range(5): cands.append(random_binary(n,rng))
    # patterns
    for period in [3,4,5,6,7,8,11,13]:
       x=np.arange(n)
       one_run=period//2
       b=(x%period < one_run).astype(float)
       if one_run==0: continue
       b=adjust_binary_sum(b,n,rng)
       cands.append(b)
    # shifted block pattern: one interval of length n/2? maybe
    b=np.zeros(n,float); b[:n//2]=1; cands.append(b)
    b=np.zeros(n,float); b[n//2:]=1; cands.append(b)
    # Keep first max_cands maybe by unique? Not needed.
    return cands[:max_cands]
```

Let's write `milp_feasible_binary` code fully.

```
def milp_feasible_binary(n, T_limit, time_limit, h_init=None):
    if n % 2 != 0: return None
    K=n//2
    T_limit=int(T_limit)
    if T_limit < 0: return None
    avg=(K*K)/(2*n-1.0)
    if T_limit < int(np.ceil(avg - 1e-12)): return None
    if h_init is not None:
       h_init=np.asarray(h_init,dtype=float)
       if h_init.size==n:
          cnt=binary_max_count(h_init)
          if cnt <= T_limit:
             return h_init
    try:
       milp = sco.milp
       LinearConstraint = sco.LinearConstraint
    except Exception:
       return None
    V=n+n*n
    rows=[]; cols=[]; vals=[]; lbs=[]; ubs=[]
    r=0; inf=np.inf
    # sum
    for i in range(n):
       rows.append(r); cols.append(i); vals.append(1.0)
    lbs.append(float(K)); ubs.append(float(K)); r+=1
    # row sums
    for i in range(n):
       zi_base=n + i*n
       for j in range(n):
          if i != j:
             rows.append(r); cols.append(zi_base+j); vals.append(1.0)
       rows.append(r); cols.append(i); vals.append(float(-K))
       lbs.append(0.0); ubs.append(0.0); r+=1
    # column sums
    for j in range(n):
       for i in range(n):
          if i != j:
             rows.append(r); cols.append(n + i*n + j); vals.append(1.0)
       rows.append(r); cols.append(j); vals.append(float(K))
       lbs.append(float(K)); ubs.append(float(K)); r+=1
    # lag constraints
    for p in range(-(n-1), n):
       if p == 0: continue
       start=max(0,-p)
       end=min(n, n-p)
       # row start
       for i in range(start,end):
          j=i+p
          rows.append(r); cols.append(n + i*n + j); vals.append(1.0)
       lbs.append(-inf); ubs.append(float(T_limit)); r+=1
    # pair constraints
    for i in range(n):
       zi_base=n+i*n
       for j in range(n):
          if i == j: continue
          z=zi_base+j
          # z - h_i <=0
          rows.append(r); cols.append(z); vals.append(1.0)
          rows.append(r); cols.append(i); vals.append(-1.0)
          lbs.append(-inf); ubs.append(0.0); r+=1
          # z + h_j <=1
          rows.append(r); cols.append(z); vals.append(1.0)
          rows.append(r); cols.append(j); vals.append(1.0)
          lbs.append(-inf); ubs.append(1.0); r+=1
          # -z + h_i - h_j <=0
          rows.append(r); cols.append(z); vals.append(-1.0)
          rows.append(r); cols.append(i); vals.append(1.0)
          rows.append(r); cols.append(j); vals.append(-1.0)
          lbs.append(-inf); ubs.append(0.0); r+=1
    A = sps.coo_matrix((vals,(rows,cols)), shape=(r,V)).tocsr()
    cons = LinearConstraint(A, np.asarray(lbs), np.asarray(ubs))
    c = np.zeros(V, dtype=float)
    bounds=[]
    for i in range(n): bounds.append((0.0,1.0))
    for i in range(n):
       base=n+i*n
       for j in range(n):
          if i == j:
             bounds.append((0.0,0.0))
          else:
             bounds.append((0.0,1.0))
    integ=np.ones(V, dtype=int)
    # x0? If h_init feasible returned. If not, no.
    options={'time_limit': int(max(1, min(3600, time_limit))), 'mip_rel_gap': 0.0, 'mip_abs_gap': 0.0}
    try:
       res=milp(c, constraints=cons, integrality=integ, bounds=bounds, options=options)
    except Exception:
       if n <= 24:
          options2={'mip_rel_gap': 0.1, 'mip_abs_gap': 1e-9}
          try:
             res=milp(c, constraints=cons, integrality=integ, bounds=bounds, options=options2)
          except Exception:
             return None
       else:
          return None
    if res is None: return None
    x=getattr(res,'x',None)
    if x is None: return None
    hres=np.rint(np.asarray(x[:n], dtype=float)).astype(int)
    if int(hres.sum()) != K:
       # try simple rounding fix to nearest K from fractional x
       hx=np.asarray(x[:n], dtype=float)
       if hx.sum() > K:
          idx=np.argsort(hx)[::-1][:int(hx.sum()-K+0.5)?]
```
This fix complicated. If sum wrong, just return None. Rare.
But `hx.sum()` fractional maybe K+0.25; int diff. Simpler:
```
       # round then adjust largest/smallest
       if hres.sum() > K:
          ones=np.flatnonzero(hres==1)
          order=np.argsort(hx[ones])[::-1] # remove smallest fractional first? Wait want reduce sum; set to 0 those with lowest hx.
          for t in range(int(hres.sum()-K)):
              hres[ones[order[t]]]=0
       else:
          zeros=np.flatnonzero(hres==0)
          order=np.argsort(hx[zeros])[::-1] # add highest fractional
          for t in range(int(K-hres.sum())):
              hres[zeros[order[t]]]=1
```

    if int(hres.sum()) != K: return None
    hresf=hres.astype(float)
    cnt=binary_max_count(hresf)
    if cnt <= T_limit and (hresf>=0).all() and (hresf<=1).all(): return hresf
    return None
```

Dir2: hi2=a+d-d=a, hj2=b-d+d=b. If fail restore h[i]=a+d, h[j]=b-d (back to current). Good. If dir1 improved and dir2 improved, h changes twice (net no? Actually applying dir2 after dir1 returns to original if fail? If improved, it keeps original values? That seems odd: dir2 (h[i]-d, h[j]+d) from improved values returns original a,b. If C improves from original? That would mean original C lower? Not possible because we didn't evaluate original after dir1? We evaluated h after dir1 and accepted; then applying dir2 returns to original h and evaluates original C. If original C > current best? def run(seed=42,budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
    t0=time.time()
    deadline=t0 + max(20.0, float(budget_s) - 5.0)
    rng=np.random.RandomState(seed)
    best_h=None; best_c=float('inf')
    try:
       parent=np.asarray(initial_h_values,dtype=float)
       if parent.size == 0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(parent)): raise ValueError
       n_parent=int(len(parent))
       best_h=project_feasible(parent,n_parent)
       best_c=compute_c5(best_h,n_parent)
    except Exception:
       n_parent=100
       best_h=np.full(n_parent,0.5,dtype=float)
       best_c=compute_c5(best_h,n_parent)
    # MIP
    if best_h is not None and time.time() < deadline - 30:
       mip_ns=[20,30,40,50,60,80,100,120]
       mip_alloc={20:30,30:60,40:60,50:80,60:80,80:90,100:90,120:70}
       for n in mip_ns:
          rem=deadline-time.time()
          if rem < 30: break
          if best_c <= 0.30: break
          alloc=mip_alloc.get(n,60)
          if best_c < 0.36: alloc=min(alloc,30)
          if rem < alloc + 15:
             alloc=max(10.0, rem-15.0)
          limit=int(np.floor(best_c * n / 2.0 - 1e-9))
          avg=(n*n/4.0)/(2*n-1.0)
          if limit < int(np.ceil(avg - 1e-12)):
             continue
          cands=candidate_binaries(n,best_h,rng,14)
          mincnt=10**9; best_cand=None
          for cb in cands:
             cnt=binary_max_count(cb)
             if cnt < mincnt:
                mincnt=cnt; best_cand=cb
          if best_cand is not None and mincnt <= limit:
             cnew=mincnt*2.0/n
             if cnew < best_c:
                best_h=best_cand.copy(); best_c=float(cnew)
          elif best_cand is not None and time.time() < deadline - 20:
             resh=milp_feasible_binary(n, limit, alloc, best_cand)
             if resh is not None:
                hres=project_feasible(resh,n) # ensure float
                cnew=compute_c5(hres,n)
                if cnew < best_c:
                   best_h=hres; best_c=float(cnew)
    # continuous
    if best_h is not None and time.time() < deadline - 5:
       n_best=int(len(best_h))
       # If best_h is not finite
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(best_h)):
          best_h=np.full(100,0.5); n_best=100; best_c=0.5
       if best_c < 0.34:
          # shorter polish
          hopt,copt=optimize_known(best_h,n_best,deadline,rng,aggressive=False)
       else:
          hopt,copt=optimize_known(best_h,n_best,deadline,rng,aggressive=True)
       if copt < best_c:
          best_h, best_c = hopt, copt
    # final ensure
    best_h=project_feasible(best_h,int(len(best_h)))
    best_c=compute_c5(best_h,int(len(best_h)))
    return best_h, float(best_c), int(len(best_h))
```

      diff=int(hres.sum())-K

```
for step in step_sizes:
    step_improved=False
    for _ in range(max_iter):
       if time.time()>deadline: return ...
       # compute cval? cval should be current; initially set. After update, cval updated.
       maxc = float(np.max(cval))
       active_tol=1e-3
       active = np.where(cval >= maxc - active_tol)[0]
       if active.size ==0: active=np.array([int(np.argmax(cval))])
       if active.size > 50:
           active = active[np.argsort(cval[active])][::-1][:50]
       else:
           active = active[np.argsort(cval[active])][::-1]
       # precompute grads
       grads=[]
       for idx in active:
           p=int(idx-(n-1)); grads.append(grad_one_lag(h,p,n))
       improved=False
       k_choices=[]
       for kk in (1,2,4,8,12,20, active.size):
           if kk>0 and kk<=active.size and kk not in k_choices: k_choices.append(kk)
       for k in k_choices:
           A=[]
           for g in grads[:k]:
              row=np.zeros(n+1); row[:n]=g; row[n]=1.0; A.append(row)
           if not A: continue
           A=np.array(A,bdtype?) (no bdtype)

           c_obj=np.zeros(n+1); c_obj[n]=-1.0

           for i in range(n): ...

           bounds.append((-step,step))

       if improved:

          # continue inner to try more with same step

    # after inner for, if step_improved and maybe no more? continue to next step? We can just proceed to next step.

```python
import time
import numpy as np
import scipy.optimize as sco
import scipy.sparse as sps
from scipy.optimize import minimize, linprog


def compute_c5_values(h, n):
    dx = 2.0 / float(n)
    return np.correlate(np.asarray(h, dtype=float), 1.0 - np.asarray(h, dtype=float), mode="full") * dx


def compute_c5(h, n):
    return float(np.max(compute_c5_values(h, n)))


def project_feasible(h, n):
    x = np.clip(np.asarray(h, dtype=float), 0.0, 1.0)
    target = float(n) / 2.0
    s = float(x.sum())
    if abs(s - target) < 1e-12:
        return x

    lo = -1.0
    hi = 1.0
    for _ in range(90):
        mid = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
        f = float(np.clip(x + mid, 0.0, 1.0).sum())
        if f < target:
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid

    y = np.clip(x + 0.5 * (lo + hi), 0.0, 1.0)
    diff = target - float(y.sum())
    if abs(diff) > 1e-11:
        if diff > 0.0:
            idx = np.flatnonzero(y < 1.0)
        else:
            idx = np.flatnonzero(y > 0.0)
        if idx.size > 0:
            if diff > 0.0:
                room = 1.0 - y[idx]
                tot = float(room.sum())
                if tot > 1e-15:
                    y[idx] += diff * room / tot
            else:
                room = y[idx]
                tot = float(room.sum())
                if tot > 1e-15:
                    y[idx] += diff * room / tot

    y = np.clip(y, 0.0, 1.0)
    diff = target - float(y.sum())
    if abs(diff) > 1e-13:
        if diff > 0.0:
            idx = np.flatnonzero(y < 1.0)
        else:
            idx = np.flatnonzero(y > 0.0)
        if idx.size > 0:
            if diff > 0.0:
                pick = idx[np.argmax(1.0 - y[idx])]
            else:
                pick = idx[np.argmax(y[idx])]
            y[pick] += diff
            y[pick] = min(1.0, max(0.0, y[pick]))
    return y


def sum_constraint(x, n):
    return float(np.sum(x)) - n / 2.0


def sum_constraint_jac(x, n):
    return np.ones(n, dtype=float)


def weighted_lag_gradient(h, weights, n):
    dx = 2.0 / float(n)
    A = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    B = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    C = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    off = n - 1
    for idx in range(2 * n - 1):
        w = float(weights[idx])
        if w <= 1e-15:
            continue
        p = idx - off
        if p >= 0:
            L = n - p
            if L <= 0:
                continue
            A[:L] += w
            B[:L] += w * h[p:n]
            C[p:n] += w * h[:L]
        else:
            q = -p
            L = n - q
            A[q:n] += w
            B[q:n] += w * h[:L]
            C[:L] += w * h[q:n]
    return dx * (A - B - C)


def soft_objective_grad(h, n, alpha):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    c = compute_c5_values(h, n)
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if alpha <= 0.0:
        return m, np.zeros(n, dtype=float)

    z = alpha * (c - m)
    ex = np.exp(z)
    s = float(np.sum(ex))
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        weights = np.zeros(c.shape, dtype=float)
        weights[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
    else:
        weights = ex / s

    grad = alpha * weighted_lag_gradient(h, weights, n)
    val = m + float(np.log(s)) / alpha
    return val, grad


def soft_objective(x, n, alpha):
    v, _ = soft_objective_grad(x, n, alpha)
    return v


def soft_jacobian(x, n, alpha):
    _, g = soft_objective_grad(x, n, alpha)
    return g


def slsqp_optimize(h0, n, alpha, maxiter=100):
    h0 = project_feasible(h0, n)
    bounds = [(0.0, 1.0)] * n
    cons = [
        {
            "type": "eq",
            "fun": sum_constraint,
            "jac": sum_constraint_jac,
            "args": (n,),
        }
    ]
    try:
        res = minimize(
            soft_objective,
            h0,
            args=(n, alpha),
            method="SLSQP",
            bounds=bounds,
            constraints=cons,
            jac=soft_jacobian,
            options={"maxiter": int(maxiter), "ftol": 1e-12, "disp": 0},
        )
        if getattr(res, "x", None) is not None:
            return project_feasible(res.x, n)
    except Exception:
        pass
    return h0


def grad_one_lag(h, p, n):
    g = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    dx = 2.0 / float(n)
    if p >= 0:
        L = n - p
        if L > 0:
            g[:L] += dx * (1.0 - h[p:n])
            g[p:n] -= dx * h[:L]
    else:
        q = -p
        L = n - q
        if L > 0:
            g[q:n] += dx * (1.0 - h[:L])
            g[:L] -= dx * h[q:n]
    return g


def lp_line_search(h, delta, n, current_c):
    target = float(n) / 2.0
    for tau in (1.0, 0.7, 0.4, 0.2, 0.1, 0.05, 0.02, 0.01, 0.005):
        hnew = h + tau * delta
        if float(np.max(hnew)) > 1.0 + 1e-9 or float(np.min(hnew)) < -1e-9:
            continue
        if abs(float(hnew.sum()) - target) > 1e-6:
            continue
        cnew = compute_c5(hnew, n)
        if cnew < current_c - 1e-13:
            return hnew, cnew
    return None


def active_lp_polish(h, n, deadline, max_iter=200):
    h = project_feasible(h, n)
    cval = compute_c5_values(h, n)
    best_c = float(np.max(cval))
    step_sizes = (0.02, 0.01, 0.005, 0.002, 0.001)

    for step in step_sizes:
        for _ in range(max_iter):
            if time.time() > deadline:
                break

            maxc = float(np.max(cval))
            active = np.where(cval >= maxc - 1e-3)[0]
            if active.size == 0:
                active = np.array([int(np.argmax(cval))], dtype=int)
            active = active[np.argsort(cval[active])][::-1]
            if active.size > 50:
                active = active[:50]

            grads = []
            for idx in active:
                p = int(idx - (n - 1))
                grads.append(grad_one_lag(h, p, n))

            improved = False
            k_choices = []
            for kk in (1, 2, 4, 8, 12, 20, active.size):
                if 0 < kk <= active.size and kk not in k_choices:
                    k_choices.append(int(kk))

            for k in k_choices:
                A = []
                for g in grads[:k]:
                    row = np.zeros(n + 1, dtype=float)
                    row[:n] = g
                    row[n] = 1.0
                    A.append(row)
                if not A:
                    continue
                A = np.asarray(A, dtype=float)
                b = np.zeros(A.shape[0], dtype=float)

                c_obj = np.zeros(n + 1, dtype=float)
                c_obj[n] = -1.0

                bounds = []
                ok = True
                for i in range(n):
                    lo = max(-step, -h[i])
                    hi = min(step, 1.0 - h[i])
                    if lo > hi + 1e-14:
                        ok = False
                        break
                    bounds.append((float(lo), float(hi)))
                if not ok:
                    continue
                bounds.append((float(-step), float(step)))

                A_eq = np.zeros((1, n + 1), dtype=float)
                A_eq[0, :n] = 1.0
                b_eq = np.array([0.0], dtype=float)

                try:
                    res = linprog(
                        c_obj,
                        A_ub=A,
                        b_ub=b,
                        A_eq=A_eq,
                        b_eq=b_eq,
                        bounds=bounds,
                        method="highs",
                    )
                except Exception:
                    continue

                if res is not None and getattr(res, "success", False):
                    delta = np.asarray(res.x[:n], dtype=float)
                    m_pred = float(res.x[n])
                    if delta.size == n and np.all(np.isfinite(delta)) and m_pred > 1e-8:
                        found = lp_line_search(h, delta, n, best_c)
                        if found is not None:
                            h_new, c_new = found
                            cval = compute_c5_values(h_new, n)
                            best_c = c_new
                            h = h_new
                            improved = True
                            break

            if not improved:
                break

    h = project_feasible(h, n)
    return h, compute_c5(h, n)


def gradient_polish(h, n, deadline, alphas):
    h = project_feasible(h, n)
    best_c = compute_c5(h, n)

    for alpha in alphas:
        for _ in range(80):
            if time.time() > deadline:
                return project_feasible(h, n), compute_c5(h, n)

            _, grad = soft_objective_grad(h, n, alpha)
            gmax = float(np.max(np.abs(grad)))
            if gmax < 1e-14:
                break

            improved = False
            for target_step in (0.02, 0.01, 0.005, 0.002, 0.001, 0.0005):
                hnew = project_feasible(h - (target_step / gmax) * grad, n)
                cnew = compute_c5(hnew, n)
                if cnew < best_c - 1e-13:
                    h = hnew
                    best_c = cnew
                    improved = True
                    break
            if not improved:
                break

    h = project_feasible(h, n)
    return h, compute_c5(h, n)


def pair_search(h, n, deadline, deltas, radius=15, max_evals=30000):
    h = project_feasible(h, n)
    best_c = compute_c5(h, n)
    evals = 0
    use_full = n <= 80

    for d in deltas:
        if time.time() > deadline:
            break
        for _pass in range(30):
            if time.time() > deadline or evals >= max_evals:
                break

            improved = False

            if use_full:
                for i in range(n):
                    if time.time() > deadline or evals >= max_evals:
                        break
                    for j in range(i + 1, n):
                        if time.time() > deadline or evals >= max_evals:
                            break

                        moved = False
                        hi = h[i] + d
                        hj = h[j] - d
                        if 0.0 <= hi <= 1.0 and 0.0 <= hj <= 1.0:
                            old_i = h[i]
                            old_j = h[j]
                            h[i] = hi
                            h[j] = hj
                            evals += 1
                            c = compute_c5(h, n)
                            if c < best_c - 1e-13:
                                best_c = c
                                improved = True
                                moved = True
                            else:
                                h[i] = old_i
                                h[j] = old_j

                        if not moved:
                            hi2 = h[i] - d
                            hj2 = h[j] + d
                            if 0.0 <= hi2 <= 1.0 and 0.0 <= hj2 <= 1.0:
                                old_i = h[i]
                                old_j = h[j]
                                h[i] = hi2
                                h[j] = hj2
                                evals += 1
                                c = compute_c5(h, n)
                                if c < best_c - 1e-13:
                                    best_c = c
                                    improved = True
                                else:
                                    h[i] = old_i
                                    h[j] = old_j
            else:
                for i in range(n):
                    if time.time() > deadline or evals >= max_evals:
                        break
                    jl = max(0, i - radius)
                    jh = min(n, i + radius + 1)
                    for j in range(jl, jh):
                        if time.time() > deadline or evals >= max_evals:
                            break
                        if j <= i:
                            continue

                        moved = False
                        hi = h[i] + d
                        hj = h[j] - d
                        if 0.0 <= hi <= 1.0 and 0.0 <= hj <= 1.0:
                            old_i = h[i]
                            old_j = h[j]
                            h[i] = hi
                            h[j] = hj
                            evals += 1
                            c = compute_c5(h, n)
                            if c < best_c - 1e-13:
                                best_c = c
                                improved = True
                                moved = True
                            else:
                                h[i] = old_i
                                h[j] = old_j

                        if not moved:
                            hi2 = h[i] - d
                            hj2 = h[j] + d
                            if 0.0 <= hi2 <= 1.0 and 0.0 <= hj2 <= 1.0:
                                old_i = h[i]
                                old_j = h[j]
                                h[i] = hi2
                                h[j] = hj2
                                evals += 1
                                c = compute_c5(h, n)
                                if c < best_c - 1e-13:
                                    best_c = c
                                    improved = True
                                else:
                                    h[i] = old_i
                                    h[j] = old_j

            if not improved:
                break

    h = project_feasible(h, n)
    return h, compute_c5(h, n)


def optimize_known(h0, n, deadline, rng, aggressive=True):
    h = project_feasible(h0, n)
    best_c = compute_c5(h, n)

    if time.time() < deadline - 8:
        extra = 120.0 if aggressive else 45.0
        sub_deadline = min(deadline - 8.0, time.time() + extra)
        h2, c2 = active_lp_polish(h, n, sub_deadline, max_iter=300 if aggressive else 100)
        if c2 < best_c - 1e-14:
            h, best_c = h2, c2

    if time.time() < deadline - 8:
        extra = 60.0 if aggressive else 20.0
        sub_deadline = min(deadline - 8.0, time.time() + extra)
        if aggressive:
            alphas = (3000, 10000, 50000)
        else:
            alphas = (10000,)
        h2, c2 = gradient_polish(h, n, sub_deadline, alphas)
        if c2 < best_c - 1e-14:
            h, best_c = h2, c2

    if aggressive:
        if n <= 80:
            alphas = (1000, 5000, 20000, 100000)
            maxiters = (70, 80, 80, 80)
        elif n <= 150:
            alphas = (3000, 15000)
            maxiters = (90, 90)
        else:
            alphas = (5000,)
            maxiters = (80,)

        for idx in range(len(alphas)):
            if time.time() > deadline - 12:
                break
            h2 = slsqp_optimize(h, n, alphas[idx], maxiter=maxiters[idx])
            c2 = compute_c5(h2, n)
            if c2 < best_c - 1e-13:
                h, best_c = h2, c2
    else:
        if time.time() < deadline - 15:
            h2 = slsqp_optimize(h, n, 10000, maxiter=50)
            c2 = compute_c5(h2, n)
            if c2 < best_c - 1e-13:
                h, best_c = h2, c2

    if time.time() < deadline - 4:
        h2, c2 = active_lp_polish(h, n, deadline - 1.0, max_iter=100 if aggressive else 50)
        if c2 < best_c - 1e-14:
            h, best_c = h2, c2

    if time.time() < deadline - 2:
        if n <= 80:
            h, best_c = pair_search(
                h,
                n,
                deadline - 1.0,
                (0.01, 0.005, 0.002, 0.001),
                radius=0,
                max_evals=120000,
            )
        elif n <= 200 and aggressive:
            h, best_c = pair_search(
                h,
                n,
                deadline - 1.0,
                (0.01, 0.005, 0.002),
                radius=10,
                max_evals=20000,
            )

    h = project_feasible(h, n)
    return h, compute_c5(h, n)


def binary_max_count(b):
    return int(np.max(np.correlate(np.asarray(b, dtype=float), 1.0 - np.asarray(b, dtype=float), mode="full")))


def random_binary(n, rng):
    idx = rng.choice(n, size=n // 2, replace=False)
    b = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    b[idx] = 1.0
    return b


def adjust_binary_sum(b, n, rng):
    b = (np.asarray(b, dtype=float) > 0.5).astype(float).copy()
    k = n // 2
    s = int(b.sum())
    if s == k:
        return b
    if s > k:
        idx = np.flatnonzero(b == 1.0)
        if idx.size < s - k:
            return b
        order = rng.permutation(idx.size)
        for t in range(s - k):
            b[idx[order[t]]] = 0.0
    else:
        idx = np.flatnonzero(b == 0.0)
        if idx.size < k - s:
            return b
        order = rng.permutation(idx.size)
        for t in range(k - s):
            b[idx[order[t]]] = 1.0
    return b


def binary_from_continuous(hc, n, rng):
    if hc is None or len(hc) == 0:
        return random_binary(n, rng)

    hc = np.asarray(hc, dtype=float)
    if len(hc) == n:
        x = hc.copy()
    else:
        x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, len(hc))
        x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n)
        x = np.interp(x_new, x_old, hc)

    scores = x + rng.normal(0.0, 0.1, n)
    idx = np.argsort(scores)[::-1]
    b = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    b[idx[: n // 2]] = 1.0
    return b


def candidate_binaries(n, best_h, rng, max_cands=14):
    cands = []
    cands.append(binary_from_continuous(best_h, n, rng))

    for _ in range(6):
        cands.append(random_binary(n, rng))

    for period in (3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 11, 13):
        one_run = period // 2
        if one_run <= 0:
            continue
        x = np.arange(n)
        b = (x % period < one_run).astype(float)
        b = adjust_binary_sum(b, n, rng)
        cands.append(b)

    b = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    b[: n // 2] = 1.0
    cands.append(b)

    b = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    b[n // 2:] = 1.0
    cands.append(b)

    if len(cands) > max_cands:
        cands = cands[:max_cands]
    return cands


def milp_feasible_binary(n, T_limit, time_limit, h_init=None):
    if n % 2 != 0:
        return None
    K = n // 2
    T_limit = int(T_limit)
    if T_limit < 0:
        return None

    avg = (K * K) / float(2 * n - 1)
    if T_limit < int(np.ceil(avg - 1e-12)):
        return None

    if h_init is not None:
        hb = np.asarray(h_init, dtype=float)
        if hb.size == n:
            if binary_max_count(hb) <= T_limit:
                return hb

    try:
        milp = sco.milp
        LinearConstraint = sco.LinearConstraint
    except Exception:
        return None

    V = n + n * n
    rows = []
    cols = []
    vals = []
    lbs = []
    ubs = []
    r = 0
    inf = np.inf

    for i in range(n):
        rows.append(r)
        cols.append(i)
        vals.append(1.0)
    lbs.append(float(K))
    ubs.append(float(K))
    r += 1

    for i in range(n):
        zbase = n + i * n
        for j in range(n):
            if i != j:
                rows.append(r)
                cols.append(zbase + j)
                vals.append(1.0)
        rows.append(r)
        cols.append(i)
        vals.append(float(-K))
        lbs.append(0.0)
        ubs.append(0.0)
        r += 1

    for j in range(n):
        for i in range(n):
            if i != j:
                rows.append(r)
                cols.append(n + i * n + j)
                vals.append(1.0)
        rows.append(r)
        cols.append(j)
        vals.append(float(K))
        lbs.append(float(K))
        ubs.append(float(K))
        r += 1

    for p in range(-(n - 1), n):
        if p == 0:
            continue
        start = max(0, -p)
        end = min(n, n - p)
        for i in range(start, end):
            j = i + p
            rows.append(r)
            cols.append(n + i * n + j)
            vals.append(1.0)
        lbs.append(-inf)
        ubs.append(float(T_limit))
        r += 1

    for i in range(n):
        zbase = n + i * n
        for j in range(n):
            if i == j:
                continue
            z = zbase + j

            rows.append(r)
            cols.append(z)
            vals.append(1.0)
            rows.append(r)
            cols.append(i)
            vals.append(-1.0)
            lbs.append(-inf)
            ubs.append(0.0)
            r += 1

            rows.append(r)
            cols.append(z)
            vals.append(1.0)
            rows.append(r)
            cols.append(j)
            vals.append(1.0)
            lbs.append(-inf)
            ubs.append(1.0)
            r += 1

            rows.append(r)
            cols.append(z)
            vals.append(-1.0)
            rows.append(r)
            cols.append(i)
            vals.append(1.0)
            rows.append(r)
            cols.append(j)
            vals.append(-1.0)
            lbs.append(-inf)
            ubs.append(0.0)
            r += 1

    A = sps.coo_matrix((vals, (rows, cols)), shape=(r, V)).tocsr()
    cons = LinearConstraint(A, np.asarray(lbs), np.asarray(ubs))
    c = np.zeros(V, dtype=float)

    bounds = []
    for i in range(n):
        bounds.append((0.0, 1.0))
    for i in range(n):
        zbase = n + i * n
        for j in range(n):
            if i == j:
                bounds.append((0.0, 0.0))
            else:
                bounds.append((0.0, 1.0))

    integ = np.ones(V, dtype=int)
    options = {
        "time_limit": int(max(1, min(3600, float(time_limit)))),
        "mip_rel_gap": 0.0,
        "mip_abs_gap": 0.0,
    }

    try:
        res = milp(c, constraints=cons, integrality=integ, bounds=bounds, options=options)
    except Exception:
        if n <= 24:
            options2 = {"mip_rel_gap": 0.1, "mip_abs_gap": 1e-9}
            try:
                res = milp(c, constraints=cons, integrality=integ, bounds=bounds, options=options2)
            except Exception:
                return None
        else:
            return None

    if res is None:
        return None
    x = getattr(res, "x", None)
    if x is None:
        return None

    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=float)
    hres = np.rint(x[:n]).astype(int)

    if int(hres.sum()) != K:
        try:
            hx = x[:n]
            diff = int(hres.sum()) - K
            if diff > 0:
                ones = np.flatnonzero(hres == 1)
                if ones.size >= diff:
                    remove = ones[np.argsort(hx[ones])[:diff]]
                    hres[remove] = 0
            else:
                zeros = np.flatnonzero(hres == 0)
                if zeros.size >= -diff:
                    add = zeros[np.argsort(hx[zeros])[::-1][: -diff]]
                    hres[add] = 1
        except Exception:
            return None

    if int(hres.sum()) != K:
        return None

    hres = hres.astype(float)
    if (hres >= 0.0).all() and (hres <= 1.0).all():
        if binary_max_count(hres) <= T_limit:
            return hres
    return None


def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.time()
    deadline = t0 + max(20.0, float(budget_s) - 5.0)
    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)

    best_h = None
    best_c = float("inf")

    try:
        parent = np.asarray(initial_h_values, dtype=float)
        if parent.size == 0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(parent)):
            raise ValueError
        n_parent = int(len(parent))
        best_h = project_feasible(parent, n_parent)
        best_c = compute_c5(best_h, n_parent)
    except Exception:
        best_h = np.full(100, 0.5, dtype=float)
        best_c = compute_c5(best_h, 100)

    mip_deadline = min(deadline - 250.0, t0 + 600.0)
    if best_h is not None and time.time() < mip_deadline:
        mip_ns = (20, 30, 40, 50, 60, 80, 100, 120)
        mip_alloc = {
            20: 30.0,
            30: 60.0,
            40: 60.0,
            50: 80.0,
            60: 80.0,
            80: 90.0,
            100: 90.0,
            120: 70.0,
        }

        for n in mip_ns:
            if time.time() > mip_deadline:
                break
            if best_c <= 0.30:
                break

            remaining = deadline - time.time()
            if remaining < 60.0:
                break

            alloc = float(mip_alloc.get(n, 60.0))
            if best_c < 0.36:
                alloc = min(alloc, 30.0)

            limit = int(np.floor(best_c * float(n) / 2.0 - 1e-9))
            avg = (n * n / 4.0) / (2.0 * n - 1.0)
            if limit < int(np.ceil(avg - 1e-12)):
                continue

            cands = candidate_binaries(n, best_h, rng, 14)
            best_cand = None
            mincnt = 10 ** 9
            for cb in cands:
                cnt = binary_max_count(cb)
                if cnt < mincnt:
                    mincnt = cnt
                    best_cand = cb

            if best_cand is None:
                continue

            if mincnt <= limit:
                cnew = float(mincnt) * 2.0 / float(n)
                if cnew < best_c:
                    best_h = best_cand.copy()
                    best_c = cnew
            else:
                if time.time() < mip_deadline - 20.0:
                    resh = milp_feasible_binary(n, limit, alloc, best_cand)
                    if resh is not None:
                        hres = project_feasible(resh, n)
                        cnew = compute_c5(hres, n)
                        if cnew < best_c:
                            best_h = hres
                            best_c = cnew

    if best_h is not None and time.time() < deadline - 5.0:
        n_best = int(len(best_h))
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(best_h)):
            best_h = np.full(100, 0.5, dtype=float)
            n_best = 100
            best_c = compute_c5(best_h, n_best)

        aggressive = bool(best_c >= 0.3808)
        hopt, copt = optimize_known(best_h, n_best, deadline, rng, aggressive=aggressive)
        if copt < best_c:
            best_h = hopt
            best_c = copt

    n_final = int(len(best_h))
    best_h = project_feasible(best_h, n_final)
    best_c = compute_c5(best_h, n_final)
    return best_h, float(best_c), n_final
```
